\partial:P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\partial P
, and requiring
\alpha|_{P}
to be the inclusion.

Proposition 14.3
.

With the notation and hypotheses above there is a morphism
\gamma:E\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to E^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{P\oplus\partial P}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}
for which the diagram,

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{E}}
\textstyle{E\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{F}}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{P\oplus\partial P}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{F^{\prime},}

commutes.

proof.
Assume by induction that
\gamma
is defined in
S_{<n}
, and let
x
be a basis element in
S_{n}
. Then
\partial x\in E\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{<n}}
and

\alpha\gamma\,\partial x=\partial\gamma_{F}\alpha_{E}x.

Since
\gamma\partial x
is a cycle and
\alpha
is a quasi-isomorphism it follows that

\gamma\partial x=\partial y,\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{some }y\in E^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{P\oplus\partial P}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}.

In particular,
\partial\alpha y=\gamma_{F}\alpha_{E}\partial x=\partial\gamma_{F}\alpha_{E}x
. Since
\alpha
is a quasi-isomorphism

\alpha y-\gamma_{F}\alpha_{E}x=\alpha z+\partial w

for some cycle
z
and some element
w\in F^{\prime}
. But necessarily deg
\,w\geq 1
, so that
w=\alpha(u)
for some
u\in P
. Thus

\alpha(y-z-\partial u)=\gamma_{F}\alpha_{E}x,

and we may extend
\gamma
by setting
\gamma x=y-z-\partial u
.

\square

Proposition 14.4
.

Suppose

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}}

is a commutative diagram connecting two profree extensions. If any two of
\varphi,\psi
and
\chi
are quasi-isomorphisms, then so is the third.

proof.
We proceed in a number of Steps.

Step One. If
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}
and
\psi=\varphi\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}}
, then
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism.

If
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism it follows from the associated spectral sequence that
\varphi\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

In the reverse direction, suppose
\varphi\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}}
is a quasi-isomorphism. Factor
\varphi
as (Proposition
14.2
)

L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L^{\prime}

in which
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
is a minimal profree extension. This then extends to

L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S},

in which the second morphism is a quasi-isomorphism by the argument above. Since the hypothesis
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}
is also a quasi-isomorphism, it follows that

L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}=L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}

is also a quasi-isomorphism.

If
T=T_{\geq n}
a simple argument then shows that
T_{n}\subset\partial(T_{n+1})+I
where
I
is the ideal generated by
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}
. This contradicts the minimality of the extension
L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
.

Step Two. If
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism then
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if
\chi
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proposition
14.2
provides a quasi-isomorphism
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}0
from a minimal profree extension. If
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism it follows from Step One that
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
and so
H(L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})=0
. This implies that the inclusions

L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\longrightarrow L\,\widehat{\amalg}\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 5.69054ptL^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\longrightarrow L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}

extend to isomorphisms

(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S},\partial_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}=L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}

and

(L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}},\partial_{S^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}=L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}

Now by Step One,
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if
\psi\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}}
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since
H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})=0=H(L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
the isomorphisms above show that this holds if and only if
\chi
is an isomorphism.

Step Three. Completion of the proof.

Here we may assume that
\psi
and
\chi
are quasi-isomorphisms, and we have to show that
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism. Now Proposition
14.2
extends
\varphi
to a quasi-isomorphism

\alpha:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L^{\prime}

from a minimal profree extension. This gives the commutative diagram,

\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{U}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}},}

in which
\gamma
is the inclusion
L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{U}
. Since
\alpha
and
\chi
are quasi-isomorphisms, it follows from Step Two that
\beta
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism, so is
\gamma\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}}
. By Step One,
\gamma
is also a quasi-isomorphism. It is straightforward to see that, since
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{U}
is a minimal extension, this implies that
U=0
and hence that
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism.

\square

Proposition 14.5
.

Any quasi-isomorphism
f:(L,\partial)\to(L^{\prime},\partial)
of enriched dgl’s extends to a surjective quasi-isomorphism

\widehat{f}:(L,\partial)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)\to(L^{\prime},\partial)

from a profree extension for which
H(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},{\partial})=0
.

proof.
Let
P
be a complete enriched vector space isomorphic with
L^{\prime}_{\geq 1}
and let
T=P\oplus\partial P
with
\partial:P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\partial P
. Use the inclusion
P\to L^{\prime}
to extend
f
to a morphism

\widehat{f}:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to L^{\prime}.

Since (cf Proposition
13.3
)
H(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})=0
it follows that the inclusion
i:L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Finally, by construction
\widehat{f}
is surjective in degrees
\geq 1
. Since
H(f)
is an isomorphism an element
x^{\prime}\in L^{\prime}_{0}
has the form
x^{\prime}=f(x)+\partial y
for some
x\in L
and
y\in L_{1}^{\prime}
. Since
y\in
Im
\,\widehat{f}
it follows that
\widehat{f}
is surjective in degree
0
.
\square

Finally, if
(L,\partial)\to(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S},\partial)
is a profree extension then division by the ideal
I
generated by
L
is a surjection

(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S},\partial)\to(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S},\partial_{S})

of enriched dgl’s. The corresponding morphism of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras is an inclusion
\land W\leftarrow\land V_{S}
. It decomposes
\land W
as a Sullivan extension

\land W=\land V_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}}\otimes\land Z.

In particular, this gives the short exact sequence,

0\to Z^{\vee}\to W^{\vee}\to V_{S}^{\vee}\to 0,

and this establishes (cf. Proposition
12.1
(iv))

Proposition 14.6
.

With the hypotheses and notation above, the quotient
\land Z
is the semi-quadratic Sullivan model of the ideal
I
.

14.2
Cylinder objects and homotopy

Important examples of a profree extension are the cylinder objects defined next, which we use to define homotopy.

Definition.

1.

A
cylinder object
for an enriched dgl,
(L,\partial)
is a commutative diagram of enriched dgl morphisms:

\textstyle{(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L,\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S},\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{(L,\partial)}

with the following properties:

\bullet

i
is a profree extension

\bullet

p
is the identity in
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,0
and in
0\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L
.

\bullet

q
is a quasi-isomorphism and
q(S)=0
.

2.

Two morphisms
f_{1},f_{2}:(L,\partial)\to(L^{\prime},\partial^{\prime})
of enriched dgl’s are
homotopic
if
f_{1}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,f_{2}:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\to L^{\prime}
extend to a morphism (called the
homotopy
),

L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L^{\prime},

from a cylinder object.

Proposition 14.7
.

Any enriched dgl
(L,\partial)
has a cylinder object.

proof.
We first establish the Proposition when
L=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
is profree, and in this case we set
S=sT
. For convenience we denote
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{\leq n}}
by
L(n)
and for
x\in L
denote by
x_{\ell}
and
x_{r}
the corresponding elements in
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,0
and
0\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L
. Now suppose by induction we have constructed
\partial
and
q
in
L(n)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(n)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{\leq n+1}}
.

Then for
x\in T_{n+1}
we have
q(\partial x_{\ell}-\partial x_{r})=0
. Since
q
is a surjective quasi-isomorphism it follows, exactly as in the proof of Proposition
13.1
(ii), that for some coherent map
\omega:L(n)\to L(n)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(n)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{\leq n+1}}
, we have

\partial\omega(x)=\partial x_{\ell}-\partial x_{r}\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }q\circ\omega=0.

Extend
q
and
\partial
to
L(n+1)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(n+1)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{\leq n+2}}
by setting

\partial\omega(x)=x_{\ell}-x_{r}-\omega\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }q(x)=0.

Now for a general enriched dgl,
(L,\partial)
, Proposition
13.1
provides a surjective quasi-isomorphism
f:(\overline{\mathbb{L}},\partial)\to(L,\partial)
from a profree dgl. Now it follows from Proposition
14.1
that
f
transforms the diagram for
\overline{\mathbb{L}}
to a diagram

\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{L}

satisfying the conditions of the Proposition.

\square

Proposition 14.8
.

(i)

Homotopy is an equivalence relation, and is independent of the choice of cylinder object.

(ii)

If
f_{1}\sim f_{1}:(L,\partial)\to(L^{\prime},\partial)
, and
g_{1}\sim g_{2}:(L^{\prime},\partial)\to(L^{\prime\prime},\partial)
then
g_{1}\circ f_{1}\sim g_{2}\sim f_{2}
.

proof.
Suppose
f:L\to L^{\prime}
is a morphism of enriched dgl’s and that
q:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L
and
q^{\prime}:L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}\to L^{\prime}
are cylinder objects. Then Proposition
14.1
(iv) yields the commutative diagram

\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f\,\widehat{\amalg}\,f}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{\prime}}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f\circ q}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{h}}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}}

which extends
f\,\widehat{\amalg}\,f
to a morphism of the cylinder objects.

(i) The commutative diagram above, applied to
id_{L}
provides a quasi-isomorphism connecting any two cylinder objects for an enriched dgl. Now it follows in the standard way that homotopy is independent of the choice of cylinder object.

On the other hand, the automorphism of
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L
exchanging the two factors extends
to the cylinder objects, and it follows that homotopy is symmetric. Moreover, denote by
L(1)
,
L(2)
and
L(3)
three copies of an enriched dgl,
L
, and let

L(1)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(2)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{and }L(2)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(3)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S(2)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L

be cylinder objects. Then Proposition
14.1
(iv) yields the commutative diagram,

\textstyle{L(1)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(2)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(3)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S(1)}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S(2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L}
\textstyle{L(1)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L(3)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

It follows that homotopy is transitive.

(ii) If
g_{1}\sim g_{2}
via
\widehat{g}:L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S^{\prime}}\to L^{\prime\prime}
then composing
g
with the morphism
\widehat{h}
in the diagram above yields a homotopy
g_{1}\circ f\sim g_{2}\circ f
. On the other hand composing
g_{i}
with the homotopy
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L^{\prime}
from
f_{1}
to
f_{2}
yields a homotopy
g_{i}\circ f_{1}\sim g_{i}\circ f_{2}
.
\square

Definition.
The set of morphisms equivalent via homotopy to a morphism
f:(L,\partial)\to(L^{\prime},\partial^{\prime})
of enriched dgl’s is its
homotopy class
and is denoted by
[f]
.

The set of homotopy classes of these morphisms is denoted by
[L,L^{\prime}]
.

Proposition 14.9
.

(i)

If
f_{1}\sim f_{2}:(L,\partial)\to(L^{\prime},\partial^{\prime})
then
H(f_{1})=H(f_{2})
. Moreover, if
L=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
and
L^{\prime}=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T^{\prime}}
then the quotient maps
\overline{f_{1}},\overline{f_{2}}:(T,\overline{\partial})\to(T^{\prime},\overline{\partial^{\prime}}
are homotopic.

(ii)

The bijection
dgl(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}dgl(L,L^{\prime\prime})\times dgl(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime})
(cf. Proposition
12.6
) induces a bijection

[L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}[L,L^{\prime\prime}]\times[L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}].

(iii)

If
(L,\partial)
is a profree dgl and
h:(L^{\prime},\partial^{\prime})\to(L^{\prime\prime},\partial^{\prime\prime})
is a quasi-isomorphism of enriched dgl’s, then composition with
h
induces a bijection

[L,L^{\prime}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}[L,L^{\prime\prime}].

(iv)

A morphism between profree dgl’s is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if it is a homotopy equivalence. Moreover, a homotopy equivalence between minimal profree dgl’s is an isomorphism.

proof.
(i) The left and right inclusions of
L
in a cylinder object
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}
are both left inverses to
q:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L
. They therefore induce isomorphisms in homology which coincide.

Then let
\psi:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T^{\prime}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T^{\prime}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
be a homotopy. Division on both sides by
(\,)^{(2)}
yields a linear map

\overline{\psi}:T^{\prime}\oplus T^{\prime}\oplus sT^{\prime}\to T
, and

\overline{\partial}\overline{\psi}sx)=\overline{f_{1}}(\overline{\partial}x)-\overline{f_{2}}(\overline{\partial}x).

(ii) This is immediate from the definition.

(iii) We show first that
[L,L^{\prime}]\to[L,L^{\prime\prime}]
is injective. For this, suppose
f_{1},f_{2}:L\to L^{\prime}
and that
h\circ f_{1}\sim h\circ f_{2}
. Then use
Proposition
14.5
to extend
h
to a surjective quasi-isomorphism,

\widehat{h}:L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}L^{\prime\prime},

in which
\partial:T\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
and
H(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)=0
. Next, let
i:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}
be a cylinder object for
L
. Then combining the hypothesis
h\circ f_{1}\sim h\circ f_{2}
with Proposition
14.1
(iv) yields the commutative diagram

\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,f_{2}}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{h}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{q}}
\textstyle{L^{\prime\prime}.}

Finally, division by the ideal generated by
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
converts
\widehat{q}
to a morphism
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L^{\prime}
. This shows that
f_{1}\sim f_{2}
.

To show that composition with
h
induces a surjection, let
g:L\to L^{\prime\prime}
be a morphism. As above, Propositions
14.1
(iv) and
14.5
yield a commutative diagram

\textstyle{L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{h}}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{g}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{L^{\prime\prime}.}

On the other hand, division by the ideal generated by
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
yields a morphism
p:L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to L^{\prime}
which is a right inverse to the inclusion
j:L^{\prime}\to L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
.

In particular,
p
is a surjective quasi-isomorphism, and since
(p\circ j\circ p)\circ\widehat{g}=p\circ\widehat{g}
it follows from the injectivity established above that

j\circ p\circ\widehat{g}\sim\widehat{g}.

But this gives

g=\widehat{h}\circ\widehat{g}\sim\widehat{h}\circ j\circ p\circ\widehat{g}=h\circ p\circ\widehat{g}.

In particular
g
is homotopic to a morphism in the image of composition with
h
.

(iv) The first assertion follows immediately from (i) and (iii) above. The second then follows immediately from Proposition
13.3
.
\square

14.3
Sullivan homotopy and semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras

A
Sullivan cylinder object
for an augmented cdga,
\varepsilon:A\to\mathbb{Q}
, is a decomposition of
(id,id):A\to A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}A
as

A\to A\otimes\land(S^{\geq 0},dS)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\to}}A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}A

in which
d:S\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}dS
and
\rho
is surjective. In particular the morphism
(\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{1}):A\otimes\land(t,dt)\to A
defined by
\varepsilon_{0}(t)=0
,
\varepsilon_{1}(t)=1
, is a Sullivan cylinder object. It is straightforward to check that two Sullivan cylinder objects for
A
are connected by a quasi-isomorphism,
\varphi
, extending
id_{A}
and satisfying
\rho^{\prime}\circ\varphi=\rho
.

Two augmentation preserving morphisms
\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}:A^{\prime}\to A
are
homotopic

(\varphi_{0}\sim\varphi_{1}
) if
(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}):A^{\prime}\to A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}A
lifts to a morphism (called the
homotopy
),

\varphi:A^{\prime}\to A\otimes\land(S,dS),

to a Sullivan cylinder object such that
\rho\circ\varphi=(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1})
. The remarks above show that the condition is independent of the choice of cylinder object.

Now recall that a morphism

\varphi:(\land V^{\prime},d_{0}+d_{1})\to(\land V,d_{0}+d_{1}),

of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras is bihomogeneous if
\varphi:V^{\prime}\to V
.

Proposition 14.10
.

Let
\land V
and
\land V^{\prime}
be the semi-quadratic models of profree dgl’s
L
and
L^{\prime}
.

(i)

The correspondence
dgl(L^{\prime},L)\mapsto cdga(\land V,\land V^{\prime})
induces a bijection

[L^{\prime},L]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}[\land V,\land V^{\prime}].

(ii)

A morphism
f:L^{\prime}\to L
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if the corresponding morphism
\land V\to\land V^{\prime}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

proof.
(i). For simplicity, we denote
d_{0}+d_{1}
simply by
d
. Then (i) follows immediately from the following two assertions:

(a)

Any morphism
\varphi:\land V^{\prime}\to\land V
is homotopic to a bihomogeneous morphism.

(b)

If
\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}:\land V^{\prime}\to\land V
are bihomogeneous morphisms corresponding to enriched dgl morphisms
f_{0},f_{1}:L^{\prime}\leftarrow L
then

\varphi_{0}\sim\varphi_{1}\Longleftrightarrow f_{0}\sim f_{1}.

proof of
(a)
. It is sufficient to consider the case that
\land V^{\prime}=\land W\otimes\land Z
in which
d:Z\to\land W
and
\varphi|_{\land W}
is bihomogeneous, and then to construct

\psi:\land W\otimes\land Z\to\land V\otimes\land(t,dt)

so that
\psi|_{\land W}=\varphi
,
\varepsilon_{0}\circ\psi=\varphi
, and
\varepsilon_{1}\circ\varphi:Z\to V
.

Fix a basis of
Z
and let
z
be an element of that basis. Since
\land V^{\prime}
is semi-quadratic and
\varphi_{|\land W}
is bihomogeneous, it follows that
\varphi(dz)
is a cycle in
\land^{\leq 2}V
. In particular the component of
\varphi(dz)
in
\land^{2}V
is a
d_{1}
-cycle. Since
L
is profree it follows from Theorem 1 that that component is a
d_{1}
-boundary : for some
v_{1}\in V
:

\varphi(dz)-d_{1}v_{1}\in V.

This gives
d\varphi(z)-dv_{1}\in V
. Thus
d\varphi(z)-dv_{1}
is a cycle in
V
that is a boundary in
\land V
. Since
L
is profree it follows from Proposition
13.2
that for some
v_{2}\in V\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{1}
,

d(\varphi z-v_{1}-v_{2})=0.

But, again by Proposition
12.2
, any cycle in
\land V
is homologous to some cycle
v_{3}\in V
, and so

\varphi z=v_{1}+v_{2}+v_{3}+d\Phi.

Define
\psi:\land W\otimes\land Z\to\land V\otimes\land(t,dt)
by

\psi w=w\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{and }\psi(z)=\varphi(z)-d(t\Phi).

proof of (b)
. Let
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}
be a cylinder object for
L
. The inclusions
i_{0},i_{1}:L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}
and the quasi-isomorphism
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L
correspond to bihomogeneous morphisms

\textstyle{\land V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda}
\textstyle{\land R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho=(\rho_{0},\rho_{1})}
\textstyle{\land V\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\land V}

of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras. Here
\land V
is a retract of
\land R
,
\rho
is surjective and (Proposition
13.3
)
\lambda
is a quasi-isomorphism. It follows that
\land R=\land V\otimes\land(S\oplus dS)
is a cylinder object for
\land V
.

Now if
f_{0}\sim f_{1}:L\to L^{\prime}
, the homotopy
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\to L^{\prime}
corresponds to a bihomogeneous morphism
\varphi:\land V^{\prime}\to\land R
. It is immediate from the definitions that
\varphi
is a homotopy from
\varphi_{0}
to
\varphi_{1}
. On the other hand, suppose
\varphi:\land V^{\prime}\to\land R
is a (not necessarily bihomogeneous) homotopy from
\varphi_{0}
to
\varphi_{1}
. Since
\varphi_{0}
and
\varphi_{1}
are bihomogeneous it is then straightforward to check that a bihomogeneous homotopy,
\widehat{\varphi}:\land V^{\prime}\to\land R
is defined by

\widehat{\varphi}:V^{\prime}\to R\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{and }\varphi-\widehat{\varphi}:V^{\prime}\to\land^{\geq 2}R.

This then corresponds to a homotopy
f_{0}\sim f_{1}.

(ii). This follows immediately from the Corollary to Proposition
13.3
.

\square

Corollary.
A morphism
\varphi:\land V\to\land V^{\prime}
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if it corresponds to a homotopy equivalence
f:L\to L^{\prime}
.

14.4
The profree dgl model of an enriched dgl

Definition.
A (minimal) profree dgl model of an enriched dgl
(L,\partial)
is a quasi-isomorphism

f:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(L,\partial)

from a (minimal) profree dgl.

Theorem 3
.

(i)

Each enriched dgl,
(L,\partial)
, has a minimal profree dgl model.

(ii)

Two profree dgl models
f(1),f(2):\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)},\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(2)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}L
determine a unique homotopy class of homotopy equivalences

g:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(2)}

such that
f(2)\circ g\sim f(1)
. If the models are minimal, then
g
is an isomorphism.

(iii)

More generally, suppose
f(1):\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}\to L(1)
and
f(2):\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(2)}\to L(2)
are profree dgl models. Then any morphism

h:L(1)\to L(2)

of enriched dgl’s determines a unique homotopy class of morphisms
\widehat{h}:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(2)}
such that

f(2)\circ\widehat{h}\sim h\circ f(1).

proof.
(i) Proposition
13.1
provides a quasi-isomorphism

\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to L

from a minimal profree dgl.

(ii) By Proposition
14.9
(iii) composition with
f
yields a bijection

[\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)},\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(2)}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}[\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)},L(2)].

This proves the existence and uniqueness, up to homotopy, of a homotopy equivalence,
g
. Proposition
14.9
(iv) asserts that if the models are minimal then
g
is an isomorphism.

(iii) This follows from Proposition
14.9
in exactly the same way as (ii).
\square

Part V
The profree dgl model of a topological space

As described in §9, Sullivan’s rational completion
X\to X_{\mathbb{Q}}
is constructed from the minimal model of
A_{PL}(X)
.
Here we provide an alternative, but equivalent, characterization of
X_{\mathbb{Q}}
in terms of minimal profree dgl’s. Namely, to any rational cdga
(A,d)
with
H^{0}(A)=\mathbb{Q}
we associate a unique (up to isomorphism) minimal profree dgl
L=(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)
together with a dga quasi-isomorphism

\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(A,d)

from its semi-quadratic Sullivan model. Thus
\land V_{L}
is a (usually non-minimal) Sullivan model of
(A,d)
. In particular, in the case of
A_{PL}(X)
,

\langle\land V_{L}\rangle\simeq X_{\mathbb{Q}}\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{and }H(\land V_{L})\cong H(X).

Remark.
Sullivan algebras
\land V
are free commutative graded algebras equipped with a differential. Profree Lie algebras are the completions (§6) of free graded Lie algebras, and profree dgl’s are profree Lie algebras equipped with a differential.

15
The profree dgl model of a cdga

Let (§12)
\land V_{L}
denote the semi-quadratic model of an enriched dgl,
(L,\partial)
. We will usually suppress the
\partial
from the notation and refer simply to an enriched dgl,
L
.

Definition.
A
(profree) enriched dgl model
of a cdga
A
is a (profree) enriched dgl,
L
, together with a quasi-isomorphism

\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A.

If
L^{\prime}
is an enriched dgl model of a cdga
A^{\prime}
then a morphism
\ell:L\to L^{\prime}
is a
dgl representative
of a homomorphism
\alpha:A^{\prime}\to A
if the morphism
\lambda(\ell):\land V_{L^{\prime}}\to\land V_{L}
corresponding to
\ell
makes the diagram

\textstyle{\land V_{L^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda(\ell)}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{A}

homotopy commutative.

In this case we also say that
A
is a cdga model of
L
and that
\alpha
is a
cdga representative of
\ell
.

The main step in establishing the introduction above is then Theorem
4
, below, which depends crucially on §14.

Theorem 4
.

(i)

Any cdga,
A
with
H^{0}(A)=\mathbb{Q}
has a minimal profree dgl model,
L
.

(ii)

If
L
and
L^{\prime}
are both profree dgl models of
A
then there is a unique homotopy class of homotopy equivalences,
f:(L^{\prime},\partial)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(L,\partial)
such that the diagram

\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{\land V_{L^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

homotopy commutes. If
L
and
L^{\prime}
are minimal then
f
is an isomorphism.

(iii)

Suppose
L
and
L^{\prime}
are profree dgl models of cdga’s
A
and
A^{\prime}
. Then any cdga morphism
\varphi:A\to A^{\prime}
determines a unique homotopy class of morphisms
f:(L^{\prime},\partial)\to(L,\partial)
such that the corresponding cdga diagram

\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\land V_{L^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}}

homotopy commutes. Moreover,
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if
f
is a homotopy equivalence.

proof.
When (i) is established, (ii) and (iii) follow immediately from §14.3, §14.4 and Proposition
12.1
.

For the proof of (i), first observe that if there is a quasi-isomorphism
\varphi:(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})\to A
from a semi-quadratic Sullivan algebra then there is also a quasi-isomorphism of the form
\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A
, in which
(L,\partial)
is a minimal profree dgl. In fact (Proposition
12.1
),
(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})
is the semi-quadratic Sullivan model of an enriched dgl,
E
. Proposition
14.2
then yields a quasi-isomorphism

L=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}E

from a minimal profree dgl. By Proposition
12.1
, this yields a quasi-isomorphism

(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\land V_{L}

of Sullivan algebras. Since such morphisms have homotopy inverses we obtain a quasi-isomorphism

\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A.

It remains to construct a quasi-isomorphism of the form
(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})\to(A,d)
. Since
H^{0}(A)=\mathbb{Q}
we may without loss of generality suppose that
A=\mathbb{Q}\oplus A^{\geq 1}
.

To start, let
Z\subset A^{\geq 1}
be a subspace of cycles for which
Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{\geq 1}(A)
. Then set
W_{0}=Z
and define a quadratic Sullivan algebra
(\land W,d_{1})
with
W=\oplus_{n\geq 0}W_{n}
by requiring that

0\to W_{n+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\land^{2}W_{\leq n}\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{1}

be exact. It then follows from the construction and from Lemma
6.2
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}\oplus W_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}H(\land W,d_{1}).

(42)

We denote
\oplus_{i\leq n}W_{i}
by
W_{\leq n}
.

Next we construct
d_{0}
in
\land W
and a morphism
\varphi:(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})\to A
by induction on
n
, and so that
d_{0}:W_{n}\to W_{<n}
and

(i)

(d_{0}+d_{1})^{2}=0
, and

(ii)

\varphi:(\land W_{\leq n},d_{0}+d_{1})\to A
is a dga morphism.

To begin set
d_{0}=0
in
W_{0}
and let
\varphi:W_{0}=Z\hookrightarrow\mbox{ker}\,d_{A}
be the inclusion. Now suppose
\varphi
and
d_{0}
are constructed in
W_{<n}
. Then filter
\land^{2}W_{<n}
by the subspaces

F_{k}=\oplus_{i+j=k,i,j<n}W_{i}\land W_{j}.

If
w\in W_{\leq n}
and
d_{1}w\in F_{n-2}
then
w\in W_{<n}
and
\varphi w
and
d_{0}w
are defined. On the other hand, for any
w\in W_{\leq n}
,
d_{1}w\in F_{2(n-1)}
. It is therefore sufficient to define
\varphi_{0}w
and
d_{0}w
if
w\in W_{n}
and for some
k
,

\bullet

d_{1}w\in F_{k}
, and

\bullet

\varphi_{0}
and
d_{0}
are already defined in
W_{\leq n}\cap d_{1}^{-1}(F_{k-1})
.

By hypothesis,
d_{0}
is defined in
W_{<n}
. Since
d_{1}d_{0}d_{1}w=-d_{0}d_{1}^{2}w=0
,
d_{0}d_{1}w=d_{1}w_{1}
for some
w_{1}\in W_{\leq n}
. But since
d_{0}:W_{p}\to W_{p-1}
it follows that
d_{0}d_{1}w\in F_{k-1}
. Thus
d_{1}w_{1}\in F_{k-1}
, and so by hypothesis
\varphi_{0}w_{1}
and
d_{0}w_{1}
are defined. In particular

d_{1}d_{0}w_{1}=-d_{0}d_{1}w_{1}=-d_{0}^{2}d_{1}w=0.

It follows from (
42
) that
d_{0}w_{1}\in W_{0}
. On the other hand,

\varphi(d_{0}w)=-\varphi(d_{0}+d_{1})(d_{1}w-w_{1})=d_{A}\varphi(w_{1}-d_{1}w).

Since
d_{0}w\in W_{0}
it follows that
d_{0}w=0
.

This in turn implies that
(d_{0}+d_{1})(d_{1}w-w_{1})=0
and so

\varphi(d_{1}w-w_{1})=\varphi z+d_{A}\Phi

for some
z\in W_{0}
. Set

d_{0}w=-w_{1}-z\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\varphi w=\Phi

to close the induction and complete the construction.

Finally, since
W_{0}\subset\mbox{ker}(d_{0}+d_{1})
and
\varphi
induces an isomorphism
W_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}H(A)
it follows that
H(\varphi)
is surjective. On the other hand, given a homology class in
H(\land W,d_{0}+d_{1})
choose a representative cycle in
\land^{\leq k}W
with
k
minimized. If
k\geq 2
the component in
\land^{k}W
is a
d_{1}
-cycle and therefore of the form
d_{1}\Phi
with
\Phi\in\land^{k-1}W
. Subtracting
(d_{0}+d_{1})\Phi
then produces a representative cycle in
\land^{\leq k-1}W
. It follows that this cycle is an element
w\in W
in which
d_{0}w=0
and
d_{1}w=0
. But this implies that
w\in W_{0}
and thus
H(\varphi)[w]\neq 0
unless
w=0
.

\square

Corollary.
Suppose a cdga
A
admits a quasi-isomorphism from a quadratic Sullivan algebra
(\land V,d_{1})
with homotopy Lie algebra
L_{V}
. Then
(L_{V},0)
is the minimal dgl model for
A
.

proof.
In this case
(\land V,d_{1})
is a semi-quadratic model for
A
, and
(L_{V},0)
is the corresponding dgl.
\square

16
The profree dgl model of a topological space

The (profree) enriched dgl model of a topological space,
X
, is a (profree) enriched dgl model of
A_{PL}(X)
. More precisely,

Definition.
A
(profree) enriched dgl model
of a topological space
X
is a (profree) enriched dgl,
(L(X),\partial)
, together with a cdga quasi-isomorphism,

(\land V_{L(X)},d_{0}+d_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A_{PL}(X)

from its semi-quadratic Sullivan model.

16.1
Connecting spaces, dgl models, and Sullivan models

Suppose
f:Y\to X
is a continuous map between connected spaces. Then denote by

\varphi_{f}:(\land V_{Y},d_{0}+d_{1})\longleftarrow(\land V_{X},d_{0}+d_{1})

a bihomogeneous morphism of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras induced from
A_{PL}(f):A_{PL}(Y)\leftarrow A_{PL}(X)
. Then
\varphi_{f}
extends to a homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{(\land V_{Y},d_{0}+d_{1})}
\textstyle{(\land V_{X},d_{0}+d_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{f}}
\textstyle{(\land W_{Y},d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{(\land W_{X},d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{f}}

in which
\land W_{Y}
and
\land W_{X}
are minimal Sullivan models of
Y
and
X
and
\psi_{f}
is a Sullivan representative of
f
. In particular this produces the homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{\langle\land V_{Y}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\langle\varphi_{f}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle\land V_{X}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathbb{Q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\mathbb{Q}}}
\textstyle{X_{\mathbb{Q}}}

On the other hand, a dgl representative
\ell_{f}
for
f
is the morphism

\textstyle{(L(Y),\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{f}}
\textstyle{(L(X),\partial)}

defined by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{(L(Y),\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{f}}
\textstyle{(L(X),\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{s(V_{Y},d_{0})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s\varphi_{f}^{\vee}}
\textstyle{s(V_{X},d_{0})^{\vee}.}

As recalled in (
12
), passage to homology converts (
16.1
) to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H(L(Y),\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{H(\ell_{f})}
\textstyle{H(L(X),\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{L_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{f}}
\textstyle{L_{X}}

in which
L_{f}
is the morphism of homotopy Lie algebras induced by
f
. This identifies

\displaystyle\pi_{*}(f_{\mathbb{Q}})=sL_{f}.

(63)

Finally, combining Proposition
13.1
and
14.1
converts
\ell_{f}:L(Y)\to L(X)
to an equivalent minimal profree extension

\textstyle{(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{Q},\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{f}}
\textstyle{(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{Q}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)}

in which
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{Q}
is also a minimal profree dgl. Thus we may assume that
\land V_{Y}
and
\land V_{X}
are the corresponding semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras. But then Proposition
13.2
provides the commutative diagram

\textstyle{(Q,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{(Q\oplus T,\overline{\partial})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{(T,0)}
\textstyle{(\land^{\geq 1}W_{Y})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\land^{\geq 1}W_{X},d)^{\vee}.}

In particular, if
H(Y)
and
H(X)
are graded vector spaces of finite type this identifies

\to Q_{k}\to H_{n}(Q\oplus T,\partial)\to T_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H(\partial)}}{{\rightarrow}}Q_{k-1}\to

with the long exact homology sequence

\to H_{k}(Y;\mathbb{Q})\to H_{k}(X;\mathbb{Q})\to H_{k}(X,Y;\mathbb{Q})\to H_{k-1}(Y;\mathbb{Q})\to

for the pair
(X,Y)
.

Remark.
Suppose
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)
is a minimal profree dgl model of
X
. Then Proposition
13.2
gives

sT\cong H^{\geq 1}(X)^{\vee}.

Thus
H(X)
is a graded vector space of finite type if and only if
T
is a graded vector space of finite type, and then

sT\cong H_{\geq 1}(X;\mathbb{Q}).

16.2
The Hurewicz homomorphism

We continue to use the notation above and, as in §9, we denote the Hurewicz homomorphism by

hur:\pi_{*}(X)\to H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})\,.

Proposition
9.3
provides the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{*}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}(X_{\mathbb{Q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{W^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H(\xi)^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H(X)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{H(\land W)^{\vee},}

where
\xi:\land^{\geq 1}W\to W
is division by
\land^{\geq 2}W
. Moreover the discussion above replaces
W^{\vee}
by
H(V_{L},d_{0})^{\vee}
and
H(\land W)^{\vee}
by
H(\land V_{L},d_{0}+d_{1})^{\vee}
. Finally, Proposition
13.2
then converts this diagram to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{*}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{hur}
\textstyle{sH(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{sH(\rho)}
\textstyle{H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{sH(T,\overline{\partial}).}

In summary, this identifies
sH(\rho)
as the
Sullivan rationalization
of the Hurewicz homomorphism.

Proposition 16.1
.

Let
X
be a simply connected space whose homotopy Lie algebra
L=L_{X}
is profree. Then the Hurewicz map induces an injective map

hur:L^{(2)}/L^{2}\to H_{*}(X_{\mathbb{Q}}).

proof.
Let
\alpha
be an element in
L^{(2)}_{q-1}
, and write
\alpha=[g]
, with
g:S^{q}\to X_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Suppose
hur(\alpha)=0
. Then there is a simply connected finite CW complex
K
of dimension
<q
and a map
f:K\to X_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\alpha\in f_{*}\pi_{q}(K)
(
[
4
]
).

Denote by
(L_{K},\partial)
the minimal cdgl model of
K
. Thus
L_{K}=\widehat{\mathbb{L}}(W)
with
W=W^{<q-1}
. Denote by
\varphi:(L_{K},\partial)\to(L_{X},0)
be a cdgl representative of
f
. Since all the elements in
L_{X}
are cycles,
\varphi(W)
is a vector space of cycles. On the other hand by construction there is a cycle
\beta\in(L_{K})_{q-1}
such that
\varphi(\beta)
is homologous to
\alpha
, thus equal to
\alpha
. But for degree reasons
\beta
is a decomposable element in
L_{K}
and so
\alpha\in L^{2}
. This gives the result.
\square

16.3
Wedges of spheres and disks

Recall from §10.4 that if
X=\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}
is a wedge of spheres then its minimal
Sullivan model is a quadratic Sullivan algebra,
(\land V,d_{1})
and that
(\land V,d_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)
with
S\cdot S=0
.

More generally, (Theorem
1
) for any graded vector space
S=S^{\geq 1}
the minimal Sullivan model
\land V_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)
is quadratic. Moreover, its homotopy Lie algebra is a profree Lie algebra
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
and the identity
s\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}=V_{S}^{\vee}
restricts to an isomorphism
sT=S^{\vee}
. Thus
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)
is a profree dgl model of
\land V_{S}
,
and if
\land V_{S}
is the model of a wedge of spheres,
X
, then
sT=H^{\geq 1}(X)^{\vee}
.

On the other hand, the wedge
\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
of disks is contractible. Thus the zero Lie algebra is a dgl model of
\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
. Therefore if
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)
is a profree dgl model of
\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}+1}
then

(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial),\hskip 28.45274pt\partial sx=x,

is a profree dgl model of
\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
. Furthermore, the inclusion

(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial)

is a dgl representative of the inclusion
i:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}.

16.4
The quotient space,
X/Y

Again let
\varphi:Y\to X
be a map of connected spaces, but assume as well that
\varphi
is the inclusion of a sub-simplicial set, or of a sub CW complex, or of a deformation retract of an open set. In each of these case the maps

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X/Y}

provide morphisms

A_{PL}(Y,y_{0})\longleftarrow A_{PL}(X,y_{0})\longleftarrow A_{PL}(X/Y,\overline{y_{0}}).

Moreover, these morphism then induce a long exact homology sequence.

Now, as above, denote by
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{Y}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{X}}
profree dgl models of
Y
and
X
. A profree representative of
\varphi
is then a morphism
f:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{Y}}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{X}}
. Moreover (Proposition
14.2
)
f
factors as

f:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.40178pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.40178pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 38.07736pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.40178pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.40178pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 65.40178pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{Y}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.89055pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\gamma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 123.8658pt\raise-4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
