in which
\lambda
is a profree extension and
\gamma
is a quasi-isomorphism. In particular,
\lambda
is a profree representation of
\varphi
.

Proposition 16.2
.

With the hypotheses above, the surjection

\rho:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{Y}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}

is a profree representative of the map
\pi:X\to X/Y
. In particular,
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
is a profree dgl model of
X/Y
.

proof.
Denote by

\psi:\land V_{X}\to\land V_{Y}

the bihomogeneous morphism corresponding to
\lambda:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{Y}}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S_{Y}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
. Since
\lambda
is a profree representative of
\varphi
, we obtain a homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{\land V_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{PL}(\varphi)}
\textstyle{\land V_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(Y).}

Our hypothesis on
\varphi
implies that
A_{PL}(\varphi)
is surjective. Therefore (
[
19
, Proposition 3.7]
) we may assume that this diagram is commutative.

On the other hand a long exact homology sequence argument show that
A_{PL}(\pi)
restricts to a quasi-isomorphism

A_{PL}(\pi):\mbox{ker}\,A_{PL}(\varphi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A_{PL}(X/Y).

Moreover, since
\lambda
is an inclusion it follows that
\psi
is surjective. Therefore a second long exact homology sequence argument shows that ker
\,\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mbox{ker}\,A_{PL}(\varphi)
.
This yields the quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mbox{ker}\,\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A_{PL}(X/Y).

(64)

Finally, let

\land V_{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longleftarrow}}\land V_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta}}{{\longleftarrow}}\land V

be the sequence of bihomogeneous morphisms in which
\eta
corresponds to the surjection
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{S}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
. Proposition
13.2
(iii) then provides the commutative diagram

\textstyle{sS_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{S_{Y}\oplus T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{(\land^{\geq 1}V_{Y})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\land^{\geq 1}V_{X})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\land V)^{\vee}.}

A final long exact homology sequence argument then shows that

(\mbox{ker}\,\psi)^{\vee}=(\land^{\geq 1}V_{X})^{\vee}/(\land^{\geq 1}V_{Y})^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\land V)^{\vee}.

It follows that
\eta
is in fact a quasi-isomorphism

\mbox{ker}\,\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longleftarrow}}\land V.

Thus we obtain the commutative diagram,

\textstyle{\land V_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X)}
\textstyle{\land V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X/Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{A_{PL}(\varphi)}

This identifies
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
as the profree dgl model of
X/Y
and
\rho
as a profree representative of
\pi
.

\square

16.5
Topological and dgl cofibres

Suppose
f_{0},f_{1}:Y\to X_{0},X_{1}
are maps between connected spaces. Identifying
y\times\{0\}
with
f_{0}(y)
and
y\times\{1\}
with
f_{1}(y)
produces the space
X_{0}\cup_{f_{0}}\,Y\times I\,\cup_{f_{1}}X_{1}
. Then

X_{0}\cup_{Y}X_{1}:=(X_{0}\cup_{f_{0}}Y\times I\cup_{f_{1}}X_{1})/(Y\times\{1/2\})

is the
cofibre
of
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
. Here we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y\times\{1/2\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{1}\cup_{f_{1}}(Y\times[1/2,1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{0}\cup_{f_{0}}(Y\times[0,1/2])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{0}\cup_{Y}X_{1},}

in which
X_{0}\simeq X_{0}\cup_{f_{0}}(Y\times[0,1/2])
and
X_{1}\simeq X_{1}\cup_{f_{1}}(Y\times[1/2,1])
.

In parallel, suppose
\ell(0),\ell(1):L\to L(0),L(1)
are morphisms of enriched dgl’s. These factor (Proposition
14.2
) through minimal profree extensions as

L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L(0)\hskip 28.45274pt\mbox{and }L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L(1).

The corresponding
cofibre square
for
\ell(0)
and
\ell(1)
is the commutative diagram,

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)\oplus T(0)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L(1)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)},}

and
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)\oplus T(1)}
is a
dgl cofibre
for
\ell(0)
and
\ell(1)
.

Proposition 16.3
.

With the hypotheses above suppose that
\ell(0)
and
\ell(1)
are enriched dgl representatives for
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
. Then

(i)

The dgl cofibre
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)\oplus T(1)}
is an enriched dgl model for
X_{0}\cup_{Y}X_{1}
.

(ii)

The morphisms in (
16.5
) are dgl representatives for the maps in (
16.5
).

As a first Corollary we obtain the following extension of Proposition
10.2
:

Corollary
. If
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{V}
and
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{W}
are profree dgl models for
X
and
Y
, then a profree dgl model for
X\vee Y
is given by
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{V}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{W}
.

The proof of Proposition
16.3
is deferred to §16.7. First, §16.6 establishes the cdga model of a general dgl cofibre.

16.6
The cdga model of a dgl cofibre

First suppose that two pairs
\ell(0),\ell(1):L\to L(0),L(1)
and
\ell^{\prime}(0),\ell^{\prime}(1):L^{\prime}\to L(0)^{\prime},L(1)^{\prime}
are connected in a commutative diagram by morphisms

\beta,\beta(0),\beta(1):L,L(0),L(1)\to L^{\prime},L(0)^{\prime},L(1)^{\prime}.

Proposition
14.3
then provides diagrams of morphisms

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma(i)}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L(i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta(i)}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{P(i)\oplus\partial P(i)}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T^{\prime}(i)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L^{\prime}(i),}

and in which
\partial:P(i)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\partial P(i)
.

Division by
P(i)
and by
\partial P(i)
then converts
\gamma(i)
into a morphism

\alpha(i):L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)}\to L^{\prime}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)^{\prime}}

extending
\beta
. In particular, the
\alpha(i)
connect the respective cofibre squares in a commutative diagram.

Proposition 16.4
.

With the notation above suppose
\beta,\beta(0)
and
\beta(1)
are quasi-isomorphisms. Then
\alpha(0)
and
\alpha(1)
are quasi-isomorphisms and induce isomorphisms

\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)^{\prime}}.

proof.
It is immediate from the diagrams above that
\gamma(0)
and
\gamma(1)
are quasi-isomorphisms. Hence so are
\alpha(0)
and
\alpha(1)
: Thus it follows from Proposition
14.4
that
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(i)^{\prime}}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
Since these are minimal profree dgl’s the Corollary to Proposition
13.3
shows that this is an isomorphism.

\square

Corollary.
If
L\to L^{\prime}
is an isomorphism then both
\alpha(0)
and
\alpha(1)
are isomorphisms. In particular, the cofibre square of
\ell(0),\ell(1)
is uniquely determined up to isomorphism.

Next suppose again that (
16.5
) is the cofibre diagram for morphisms
\ell(0),\ell(1):L\to L(0),L(1)
. Then denote the corresponding semi-quadratic models for
L,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)},L\,\widehat{\amalg}\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}
and
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)\oplus T(1)}
respectively by
\land V_{L}
,
\land V(0),\land V(1)
and
\land V
.

Proposition 16.5
.

With the hypotheses above, the morphism

\varphi:\land V\to\land V(0)\times_{\land V_{L}}\land V(1)

resulting from (
16.5
) is a quasi-isomorphism.

proof.
First observe that the morphisms in (
16.5
) are injections and the duals of the morphisms
V\to V(0),V(1)\to V_{L}
. Thus these linear maps are surjections. It follows that
H(\land V(0))\times H(\land V(1)),H(\land V_{L})
, and
H(\land V(0)\times_{\land V_{L}}\land V(1))
are connected by a long exact homology sequence. On the other hand, Proposition
13.1
, (i) and (iii), provide a quasi-isomorphism

\psi:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L

from a minimal profree dgl,
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
. The discussion above, together with Proposition
16.4
, connects the cofibre square for
\ell(0),\ell(1)\circ\psi:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to L(0),L(1)
by quasi-isomorphisms to the cofibre square for
\ell(0),\ell(1)
. The corresponding morphisms of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras are therefore (Proposition
12.4
) also quasi-isomorphisms. Furthermore, a long exact homology sequence argument then shows that the induced map of fibre products from
\land V(0)\times_{\land V_{L}}\land V(1)
is also a quasi-isomorphism. To summarize,
this reduces Proposition
16.5
to the case
L
is a minimal profree dgl,
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
.

Finally, since
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(0)}
and
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(1)}
are minimal profree extensions, Proposition
13.2
(iii) identifies the desuspension of the dual of
H(\varphi)
as the isomorphism

\frac{T\oplus T(0)\oplus T\oplus T(1)}{T}\longrightarrow T\oplus T(0)\oplus T(1)

in which the inclusion of
T
in
T\oplus T
is the map
x\mapsto(x,-x)
.
\square

16.7
Proof of Proposition
16.3
.

Without loss of generality we may replace
X_{0},Y,X_{1}
respectively by

X_{0}^{\prime}=X_{0}\cup_{f_{0}}(Y\times[0,1/2]),\hskip 19.91692ptY^{\prime}=Y\times\{1/2\},\hskip 17.07164pt\mbox{and }X_{1}^{\prime}=X_{1}\cup_{f_{1}}(Y\times[1/2,1]).

Then we may choose Sullivan models
\land V_{X_{0}},\land V_{Y}
and
\land V_{X_{1}}
for
X_{0}^{\prime},Y^{\prime}
and
X_{1}^{\prime}
and surjective morphisms

\psi_{0},\psi_{1}:\land V_{X_{0}},\land V_{X_{1}}\to\land V_{Y}

so that
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
correspond to the inclusions
Y^{\prime}\to X_{0}^{\prime},X_{1}^{\prime}
. It then follows that
\land V_{X_{0}}\times_{\land V_{Y}}\land V_{X_{1}}
is quasi-isomorphic to
A_{PL}(X_{0}\cup_{Y}X_{1}).

On the other hand, since
\ell(0),\ell(1):L\to L(0),L(1)
are dgl representatives of the inclusions
Y\to X_{0},X_{1}
they are also dgl representatives for the inclusions
Y^{\prime}\to X_{0}^{\prime},X_{1}^{\prime}
. Thus by definition we have homotopy commutative diagrams

\textstyle{\land V(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{0}}
\textstyle{\land V_{X_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{0}}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land V_{Y}}

and
\textstyle{\land V(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\textstyle{\land V_{X_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{1}}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land V_{Y}.}

Moreover, since
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
are surjective we may choose
\sigma_{0}
and
\sigma_{1}
to make the diagrams commutative. A long exact homology sequence argument then shows that
\sigma_{0}
and
\sigma_{1}
induce a cdga quasi-isomorphism

\land V(0)\times_{\land V_{L}}\land V(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\land V_{X_{0}}\times_{\land V_{Y}}\land V_{X_{1}}\simeq A_{PL}(X_{0}\cup_{Y}X_{1}).

Thus Proposition
16.3
follows from Proposition
16.5
.

\square

17
Cell attachments

17.1
Topological cell attachments

Definition.
A (topological)
attaching map
is a continuous map

g:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X

from a wedge of connected spheres to a connected space,
X
. Then the map

\iota_{X}:X\to X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}

is a (topological)
cell attachment
.

Given such a cell attachment observe that
X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
is the cofibre of the pair of maps,

g,i:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X,\vskip 8.53581pt\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1},

and

\textstyle{\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{X}}
\textstyle{\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}}

is the corresponding commutative diagram (
16.5
).

Now denote by
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\,\widehat{\amalg}\,sT},\partial sx=x)
the dgl representative of
\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
constructed in §16.3, and let
(L,\partial_{L})
be a dgl model of
X
. Then (§16) the dgl diagram (
16.5
) corresponding to the diagram above is given by

\textstyle{(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{g}}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{X}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}.}

Thus Proposition
16.3
specializes to

Proposition 17.1
.

With the hypotheses and notation above,

(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial),\hskip 28.45274pt\partial sx=\ell_{g}(x),

is an enriched dgl model for
X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
. Furthermore, the inclusions

\ell_{g}:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to L\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 8.53581ptj_{X}:L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}

are respectively dgl representatives of
g
and of
i_{X}
.

Example.

Attachment of 2-cells to a finite wedge of circles

Suppose
g:Y=\vee_{i=1}^{r}S^{1}_{a_{i}}\to X=\vee_{j=1}^{s}S^{1}_{b_{j}}
is an attaching map with corresponding cell attachment

i_{X}:X\to X\cup_{g}\vee_{i}D^{2}_{a_{i}}.

Then
g
determines and is determined by a group homomorphism

\omega:G_{a}\to G_{b}

between the corresponding fundamental groups. Both groups are free and freely generated respectively by element
a_{1},\dots,a_{r}
and
b_{1},\dots,b_{s}
corresponding to the circles.

In particular, dgl models for
Y
and
X
respectively have the form - §16.3 -
(L_{Y},0)
and
(L_{X},0)
and we have the commutative diagram,

\textstyle{(L_{Y},0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{g}}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{(L_{X},0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{sV_{Y}^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s\psi^{\vee}}
\textstyle{sV_{X}^{\vee},}

where
\psi:(\land V_{Y},d_{1})\longleftarrow(\land V_{X},d_{1})
is the bihomogeneous morphism of the quadratic Sullivan algebra models of
Y
and
X
.

On the other hand, by Proposition
9.4

L_{Y}=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(a)}\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }L_{X}=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T(b)}

in which
T(a)
has as a basis the elements
\log_{G_{a}}(a_{i})
and
T(b)
has as a basis the elements
\log_{G_{b}}(b_{j})
. Moreover, it is immediate from the definitions (§9.1) that the diagram

\textstyle{G_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\textstyle{G_{L_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\log}
\textstyle{L_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{g}}
\textstyle{G_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{L_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\log}
\textstyle{L_{X}}

commutes. Therefore

\ell_{g}(\log_{G_{a}}(a_{i}))=\log_{G_{b}}(\omega a_{i}).

Thus it follows from Proposition
17.1
that a dgl representative of
i_{X}
is the morphism

L_{X}\to(L_{X}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT(a)},\partial)\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{with }\partial s\log_{G_{a}}a_{i}=\log_{G_{b}}(\omega a_{i}).

17.2
dgl cell attachments

Proposition
17.1
permits the generalization of topological cell attachments to general dgl cell attachments.

Definition.
A
dgl attaching map
is a morphism

\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})

of enriched dgl’s, and the corresponding
dgl cell attachment
is the inclusion

j:L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\hskip 14.22636pt\partial sx=\gamma(x).

Remark.
If
\gamma:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to L
is a dgl attachment then, since
\gamma
is coherent, both
\gamma(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
and
\gamma(T)
are closed subspaces of
L
.

17.3
Sullivan cell attachments

Sullivan algebras also admit a definition of cell attachments and this is equivalent to the definition of dgl attachments (Proposition
17.3
below). This, however, provides an explicit minimal model representation of a cell attachment (Proposition
17.4
).

In fact, as
recalled in §16.3, Theorem 1 provides for each graded vector space
S=S^{\geq 1}
a quadratic Sullivan model,

(\land V_{S},d_{1})\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)

in which
S\cdot S=0
. Moreover, (§16.3)
(\land V_{S},d_{1})
is the semi-quadratic model of the profree dgl,
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)
, with
sT=S^{\vee}
.

Definition.
A
Sullivan attaching map
is a morphism

\rho:(\land W,d)\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)

from a minimal Sullivan algebra, where
S=S^{\geq 1}
is an arbitrary such graded vector space. If
\rho
is surjective then it is a
surjective attaching map
.

Now suppose
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})
is a dgl attaching map. Then (Proposition
12.1
)
\gamma
induces a morphism

\psi:\land V_{L}\to\land V_{S}

between the semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras corresponding to
(L,\partial_{L})
and
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)
. By definition,
\psi
is a Sullivan representative for
\gamma
. A minimal Sullivan model
\land W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\land V_{L}
has a surjective homotopy inverse
\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\land W
. This (with (
12
)) then determines the commutative
diagrams

\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\land W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\land V_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{S}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,}

and
\textstyle{sT\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{sH(\gamma)}
\textstyle{S^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\vee}}
\textstyle{sH(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{W^{\vee},}

in which the first horizontal morphisms are surjective quasi-isomorphisms. In particular, there follows

Proposition 17.2
.

The map
H(\gamma):T\to H(L)
is injective if and only if
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is surjective.

Definition.

\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is the Sullivan attaching map corresponding to
\gamma
.

Remark.
Of course
\rho
lifts through
\rho_{S}
to provide a morphism
\land W\to\land V_{S}
, and this as well as
\psi
are Sullivan representatives of
\gamma
. We shall, however, abuse notation and refer to
\rho
as a Sullivan representative of
\gamma
.

Proposition 17.3
.

The correspondence
\gamma\mapsto\rho
described above defines a bijection

[\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},L]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}[\land W,\mathbb{Q}\oplus S]

from the homotopy classes of dgl attaching maps to the homotopy classes of Sullivan attaching maps.

proof.
Since
\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\land W
is a surjective quasi-isomorphism, it follows that any morphism
\land W\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S)
lifts to a morphism
\land V_{L}\to\land V_{S}
. Now Proposition
14.10
shows that this determines a unique homotopy class of dgl morphisms
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})
. These by definition induce morphisms
\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
homotopic to the original one.
\square

Remark.
The relationships among attaching maps

g:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X,\hskip 17.07164pt\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 8.53581pt\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S

can be summarized as follows:

(i)

If
\gamma
is obtained from
g
then
\gamma=\ell_{g}
is a dgl representative of
g
.

(ii)

If
\rho
is obtained from
\gamma
then
\rho
is a Sullivan representative of
\gamma
.

(iii)

If
\rho
is obtained from
\gamma
which was obtained from
g
then
\rho
is a Sullivan representative of the continuous map
g
.

Again, let
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
be the Sullivan attaching map corresponding to a dgl attaching map

\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L}).

Then
\rho
factors over the surjection
\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus W
to define a linear map

\overline{\rho}:W\to S.

This in turn defines two cdga’s:

\bullet

(A,d):=\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mbox{ker}\,\overline{\rho}\oplus\land^{\geq 2}W\subset\land W
, and

\bullet

(\mathbb{Q}\oplus s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho},0)
with
s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho}\cdot s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho}=0
.

Proposition 17.4
.

Denote by

\land V_{L}\longleftarrow\land\widehat{V}

the morphism of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras corresponding to
j:L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}
. Then there is a quasi-isomorphism,

\widehat{\varphi}:\land\widehat{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho}),

which makes the diagram

\textstyle{\land\widehat{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W}

homotopy commutative.

proof.

We shall use without future reference the following basic fact. If
C(0),C(1)\to C
are morphisms of chain complexes and if the first is surjective then the long exact homology sequence for
C(0\times_{C}C(1)
computes
H(C(0)\times_{C}C(1))
.

Next, let

\land V(0)

denote the semi-quadratic Sullivan model of
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\oplus sT},\partial)
. Thus (Proposition
12.1
) the dgl morphisms

\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\oplus sT}\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 8.53581pt\gamma\,\overline{\amalg}\,id:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}

induce morphisms

\land V(0)\to\land V_{S}\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and}\,\,\land\widehat{V}\to\land V(0).

In particular (§16.3) the quasi-isomorphism
\land V_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
extends to a quasi-isomorphism

\land V(0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{Q}\oplus S\oplus U,\hskip 14.22636ptd:U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}S.

Now (Proposition
14.2
) factor
\gamma:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to L
as

\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\oplus T(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}L.

This yields the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\oplus T(1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\oplus sT}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T\oplus T(1)\oplus sT}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}}

In view of the Remarks above and Proposition
16.5
it follows that

\land\widehat{V}\to\land V(0)\times_{\land V_{S}}\land V_{L}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Now the surjections

\land V(0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Q}\oplus S\oplus U,\hskip 11.38109pt\land V_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{and the surjection }\land V_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\land W

yield a quasi-isomorphism

\land\widehat{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(S\oplus U)\times_{S}\land W.

Write
S=\mbox{Im}\,\psi\oplus C
and denote
U_{C}=d(C)
. Then a straightforward check shows that the inclusion

(U_{C},0)\times A\to(S\oplus U)\times_{S}\land W

is a cdga quasi-isomorphism. Since
U_{C}\cong s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho}
, this completes the proof.

\square

Corollary.
Suppose
g:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X
is a topological attaching map and that

j_{X}:L(X)\to L(X)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}

is a dgl representative of
\iota_{X}:X\to X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
. Then the diagram

\textstyle{\land\widehat{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W}

identifies
A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho})\to\land W
as a cdga representative of
i_{X}
.

proof.
Since
j_{X}
is a dgl representative of
i_{X}
it follows by definition - see (
16.1
) - that

\textstyle{\land\widehat{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{PL}(i_{X})}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X)}

homotopy commutes. Thus the Corollary follows from Proposition
16.4

\square

17.4
Surjective Sullivan attaching maps

A Sullivan attaching map
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is surjective if and only if coker
\,\overline{\rho}=0
, i.e. if and only if the diagram (
17.4
) of Proposition
17.4
reduces to the homotopy commutative diagram,

\textstyle{\land V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lambda}
\textstyle{\land V_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W,}

in which
\land\widehat{V}
is replaced by its minimal Sullivan model
\land V
. Since (Proposition
12.5
)
\land V_{L}\to\land W
is surjective we may choose
\land V\to\land V_{L}
so that the diagram in fact commutes.

Thus Proposition
17.4
reduces to

Proposition 17.5
.

Suppose
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is surjective.

(i)

The morphism

\lambda:\land V\to\land W

is a Sullivan representative of
j:L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}
.

(ii)

Let
Q=s^{-1}S
and consider the cdga
(\land W\oplus Q,D)
in which

Dw=dw+s^{-1}\rho w

and
W\cdot Q=0
. Then, although
(\land W,d)\to(\land W\oplus Q,D)
is not always a cdga morphism, the composite

A\to\land W\to\land W\oplus Q

is a cdga quasi-isomorphism
(A,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(\land W\oplus Q,D)
. In particular the composite

(\land V,d)\to(\land W\oplus Q,D)

is a Sullivan model.

Now if
\rho
is surjective if follows that
H^{1}(\lambda)
is injective. Therefore
\lambda
decomposes
\land W
as a
\Lambda
-extension

\lambda:\land V\to\land V\otimes\land Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\land W

in which
Z=Z^{\geq 1}
and
\land Z
is a minimal Sullivan algebra.

This in turn provides the diagram

\textstyle{\langle\land V\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle\land V\otimes\land Z\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\langle\land Z\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\langle\land W\rangle,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\lambda\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

in which the horizontal row is a fibration (
[
17
, Proposition 17.9]
). This identifies
\langle\land Z\rangle
as the homotopy fibre of
\langle\lambda\rangle
.

On the other hand, since
\land\widehat{V}\to\land V_{L}
is by definition a Sullivan representative of
L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}
it follows that
\land V\to\land V_{L}
is also such a Sullivan representative . In particular, if
L\to L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}
is obtained from a topological cell attachment
i_{X}:X\to\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
, it follows that
\lambda
is a Sullivan representative of
i_{X}
. This establishes

Proposition 17.6
.

With the hypotheses and notation above

\langle\lambda\rangle=(i_{X})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and
\langle\land Z\rangle
is the homotopy fibre of
(i_{X})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

18
The profree dgl model and the Sullivan model of a CW complex

Fix a CW complex
X=\cup_{n}X_{n}
in which
X_{n}
denotes the
n
-skeleton and
X_{0}=\{pt\}
. Then we may write

X_{n+1}=X_{n}\cup_{g_{n}}\vee_{\alpha}D_{\alpha}^{n+1},

where
g_{n}:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n}_{\alpha}\to X_{n}
is the attaching map. By Proposition
17.1
, if
L_{X_{n}}
is a profree dgl model for
X_{n}
then
X_{n+1}
has a profree dgl model of the form

L_{X_{n}}\to(L_{X_{n}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT_{n-1}},\partial)

in which
L_{g_{n}}:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{n-1}}\to L_{X_{n}}
is a profree representative for
g_{n}
. Now the obvious induction produces a profree dgl
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},\partial)
in which
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{<n}}
is a profree dgl model for
X_{n}
and the inclusion
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
is represented by

\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{<n}}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{\leq n}}
.

Theorem 5
.

Let
X
be a CW complex as above. Then

(i)

L=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
is a profree dgl model of
X
.

(ii)

The isomorphisms
sH(T_{<n},\overline{\partial})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{\geq 1}(X_{n})^{\vee}
induce an isomorphism

sH(T,\overline{\partial})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left[H^{\geq 1}(X)\right]^{\vee}.

proof.
Denote by
L(n)
and by
\land V(n)
the dgl
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{<n}}
and its semi-quadratic Sullivan model. The inclusion
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{<n}}\to\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T_{\leq n}}
is then the dual (up to suspension) of a surjection
V(n+1)\to V(n)
. This yields the sequence of surjections

\dots\leftarrow\land V(n)\leftarrow\land V(n+1)\leftarrow\dots

of semi-quadratic Sullivan algebras.
Moreover, for
k\leq n
,

V^{k}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}V^{k}(n+1).

It follows that

\varinjlim_{n}\land V(n)=\varinjlim_{n}\land V^{\leq n}(n)=\land\left(\varinjlim_{n}V^{\leq n}(n)\right).

Now set
V=\varinjlim_{n}V^{\leq n}(n)
. Then the obstructions above imply that
\land V
is a semi-quadratic Sullivan algebra and in fact is the semi-quadratic Sullivan model of
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
. Moreover, by definition
L(n)\to L(n+1)
is a profree representative of the inclusion
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
. Thus

\textstyle{\land V(n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X_{n})}
\textstyle{\land V(n+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A_{PL}(X_{n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

is a homotopy commutative diagram. Now the same degree by degree argument shows that
\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A_{PL}(X)
. This completes the proof.
\square

Proposition 18.1
.

If
X
is a connected CW complex all of whose cells are odd dimensional then

(i)

The minimal profree model of
X
has the form
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)
with
T=T_{even}
.

(ii)

The minimal Sullivan model of
X
is also the minimal Sullivan model of a wedge of odd spheres.

proof.
(i) follows directly from Theorem
5
, while (ii) follows directly from Proposition
10.6
.

\square

18.1
Cell attachments via a wedge of spheres of finite type

Suppose
Y=\vee S^{n_{\alpha}}
is a wedge of connected spheres with only finitely many spheres in each dimension. Let
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)
be the minimal profree dgl model of
Y
(§16.3) and note that its quadratic Sullivan model is then the minimal Sullivan model of
Y
. We show now that the composite

\pi_{*}(Y)\to\pi_{*}(Y_{\mathbb{Q}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}s\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to sT

maps a set of generators,
\alpha_{i}
, of
\pi_{*}(Y)
onto a basis of
sT
. (The central isomorphism is (
16.1
) in §16).

In fact, let
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k}\in\pi_{n}(Y)
be represented by the
n
-spheres in
Y
. Then
H^{n}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
has a basis represented by the fundamental cohomology classes of the spheres, and it follows that the map
\pi_{*}(Y)\to H_{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
maps the
\alpha_{i}
to the dual of a basis
a_{i}
for
H^{n}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
. By Proposition
10.6
, the
a_{i}
are a basis of
sT
.

Therefore a morphism from
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
is uniquely determined by its composite with
\pi_{*}(Y)\to\pi_{*}(Y_{\mathbb{Q}})
. In particular, if
g:Y\to X
is any map, then from the commutative diagram (cf (
18
)

\textstyle{\pi_{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}(g)}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}(Y_{\mathbb{Q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{g}}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}(X_{\mathbb{Q}})}

we obtain that
\pi_{*}(g)
uniquely determines
L_{g}
. Therefore the profree representative of
X\to X\cup_{g}\vee D^{n_{\alpha}+1}
is given by

L_{X}\to L_{X}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\hskip 28.45274pt\partial sx=\pi_{*}(g)x,

where
\pi_{*}(g)(x)
is regarded as an element in
\pi_{*}(X_{\mathbb{Q}})
.

In particular, if
X
is a connected CW complex of finite type then the differential
\partial
in
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
in Theorem
5
can be constructed directly from the morphisms
\pi_{*}(g_{\alpha}):\pi_{*}(S^{n_{\alpha}})\to\pi_{*}(X^{n})
where the
g_{\alpha}
are the attaching maps of
X
.

Part VI
Inert attaching maps

19
dgl vs Sullivan attaching maps

Recall (§17) that a topological attaching map and the induced cell attachment are continuous maps of connected spaces

g:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 8.53581pti_{X}:X\to X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1}.

While Im
\,\pi_{*}(g)\subset
Ker
\,\pi_{*}(\iota_{X})
this construction will often create additional elements in
\pi_{*}(X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1})
and it is far from understood which conditions will assure that

\pi_{*}(i_{X}):\pi_{*}(X)\to\pi_{*}(X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1})

is surjective.

By contrast this condition is much better understood after passage to Sullivan’s rational completions: consistent with
[
3
]
and
[
24
]
we introduce the

Definition.
The attaching map,
g
, is
rationally inert
if

\pi_{*}(i_{X})_{\mathbb{Q}}:\pi_{*}(X_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\pi_{*}((X\cup_{g}\vee_{\alpha}D^{n_{\alpha}+1})_{\mathbb{Q}})

is surjective.

This can be rephrased in terms of the dgl representatives (Proposition
17.1
)

\ell_{g}:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 8.53581ptj_{X}:(L,\partial_{L})\to(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial)

of
g
and
i_{X}
. Since (
63
)
\pi_{*}(i_{X})_{\mathbb{Q}}=sH(j_{X})
it follows that
g
is rationally inert if and only if
H(j_{X})
is surjective. Therefore we can generalize the definition above to the category of enriched dgl’s:

Definition.
A dgl attaching map
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})
is
inert
if

H(j):H(L)\to H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT})

is surjective.

Thus

g:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X\mbox{ is rationally inert }\Leftrightarrow\mbox{a dgl representative of $g$ is inert}.

On the other hand, (§17.3) a Sullivan attaching map is a morphism

\rho:(\land W,d)\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)

from a minimal Sullivan algebra. Proposition
17.3
establishes a bijection between the homotopy classes of Sullivan attaching maps and the homotopy classes of dgl attaching maps
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial)
, in which (
17.3
)

\bullet

\land W
is the minimal Sullivan model of
(L,\partial)
, thereby identifying

sH(L)=W^{\vee},

\bullet

sT=S^{\vee}
and
sH(\gamma)=\rho^{\vee}
,

and

\bullet

sH(\gamma):sT\to sH(L)
.

Definition.

Such a pair
(\gamma,\rho)
of attaching maps is called
equivalent
.

Now, as in §17.3, with
\rho
we associate the cdga

(A,d):=\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mbox{ker}\,\rho|_{W}\oplus\land^{\geq 2}W.

But here we also introduce the minimal Sullivan model of
A
:

\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A,

which combined with the inclusion
A\to\land W
defines morphisms

\lambda:\land V\to\land W\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\hskip 8.53581ptL_{\lambda}:L_{V}\leftarrow L_{W},

L_{\lambda}
denoting the corresponding morphism of homotopy Lie algebras. Then we introduce the

Definition.
A Sullivan attaching map
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is
inert
if both (i)
\rho
is surjective and (ii)
L_{\lambda}
is surjective.

Proposition 19.1
.

Suppose
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial)
and
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
are equivalent attaching maps. Then

(i)

H(\gamma):T\to H(L)
is injective
\Leftrightarrow

\rho
is surjective.

(ii)

Then
\mbox{$\gamma$ is inert $\Longleftrightarrow$ $\rho$ is inert}.

(iii)

If
\gamma
is a dgl representative of
g:\vee S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X
then
\gamma
is inert if and only if
g
is rationally inert.

(iv)

If
\gamma
is inert then
H(\gamma):T\to H(L,\partial)
is injective.

proof.
(i) This is Proposition
17.2
. (ii) We rely on diagram (
17.4
) in Proposition
17.4
, which introduces the cdga
A\times_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}\oplus s\,\mbox{coker}\,\overline{\rho})
. The homotopy Lie algebra of the minimal Sullivan model of this cdga has (Proposition
12.6
) the form
L_{V}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,E
, in which
E=0
if and only if
\rho
is surjective.

On the other hand, we can now apply (
12
) to convert (
17.4
) to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial)}
\textstyle{H(L,\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H(j)}
\textstyle{L_{V}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{L_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{L_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

It follows that
H(j)
is surjective if and only if
E=0
and
L_{\lambda}
is injective.

(iii) As observed above this follows because
\pi_{*}(\iota_{X})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is surjective if and only if
H(L)\to H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT})
is surjective.

(iv) This follows from (i).

\square

Corollary.
If
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is the Sullivan representative of an attaching map
g:\vee_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha}}\to X
, then
g
is rationally inert if and only if
\rho
is inert.

Definition.
If
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial)
is an inert dgl attaching map then
H(\gamma)(T)
is an
inert subspace
of
H(L)
and its non zero elements are
inert elements
.

20
Characterisation of inert dgl attaching maps

Given a morphism
\gamma:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to E
of complete enriched Lie algebras, we denote by
I
the closed ideal generated by
\gamma(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
and by
p:E\to E/I
the quotient map. Then let
\lambda:C\to E
be the inclusion of a closed subspace for which
p\circ\lambda:C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}E/I
. This (§10.2) defines a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\lambda}
\scriptstyle{0\,\widehat{\amalg}\,p\lambda}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{E/I.}

Definition.

\gamma(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})

freely generates

I
if
\gamma\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\lambda
is an isomorphism.

Now consider a dgl attaching map
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})
. Then
\gamma
determines the profree extension

j:(L,\partial_{L})\to(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma}),\hskip 28.45274pt\partial_{\gamma}(sx)=\gamma x.

Denote by
I
the closed ideal in
H(L)
generated by
H(\gamma)(T)
.

Theorem 6
.

With the hypotheses above, the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\gamma
is inert.

(ii)

ker
\,H(j)
is freely generated by
H(\gamma)(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
.

(iii)

H(\gamma):\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to H(L)
is inert.

In particular, in this case
I=\mbox{ker}\,H(j).

proof.

Step One, the quasi-isomorphism

(L,\partial_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial),

where
\partial|_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}}=0
,
\partial|_{L}=\partial_{L}
, and
\partial|_{sT}(sx)=\gamma x-x
.

For convenience we write
\partial|_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}}=\partial_{\gamma}+\partial^{\prime}
, where
\partial_{\gamma}(sx)=\gamma x
,
\partial^{\prime}(sx)=-x
, and
\partial_{\gamma}
and
\partial^{\prime}
vanish on
\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}
and
L
.

Let
(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT})(n)
denote the subspace of iterated Lie brackets of length
\leq n
in
T
and
sT
. Then the increasing filtration by the closed subspaces
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT})(n)
provides a convergent spectral sequence whose first term is
(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}),\partial_{L}+\partial^{\prime})
.
It follows that

(L,\partial_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial)

is a quasi-isomorphism. The reverse quasi-isomorphism is then

id_{L}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\gamma\,\widehat{\amalg}\,0:L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}L.

Note that this gives the commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.16148pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.16148pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 113.69803pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{q}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 113.69803pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 42.16148pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.16148pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma}),}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\hskip 28.45274ptq|_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}}=0.

This identifies
H(q)
with
H(j)
.

Step Two. If
\gamma
is inert then
I=
ker
\,H(j)
and is freely generated by
H(\gamma)(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
.

By hypothesis,
H(q)
is surjective. Since
q
is also surjective there is a coherent linear map

\sigma:(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma})\to(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial)

such that
q\circ\sigma=id
. This in turn defines a chain map

id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\sigma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma})\to(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial).

Assign to iterated Lie brackets of
n
elements
x_{i}\in T
with elements in
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}
the filtration degree
n
. This defines an increasing filtration of both dgl’s, and the chain map of the associated graded chain complexes, induced by
id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\sigma
is just the identity. It follows that
id_{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\sigma
is an isomorphism of chain complexes. In particular it induces an isomorphism and a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\,\widehat{\amalg}\,id}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H(j)}
\textstyle{H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma}).}

This identifies ker
\,H(j)
as the ideal
I
generated by
H(\gamma)(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
and shows that the ideal is freely generated.

Step Three. If ker
\,H(j)
is freely generated by
H(\gamma)(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
then
\gamma
and
H(\gamma
) are inert.

Recall that
j
is the profree extension

j:(L,\partial_{L})\to(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},\partial_{L}+\partial_{\gamma}).

Thus
j
induces a morphism of spectral sequences, where
L
has the trivial spectral sequence and
L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}
has the spectral sequence induced from the secondary gradation. At the
E_{1}
level this gives the morphism

H(L)\to(H(L)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},H(\partial_{\gamma})).

Since
H(L)
is freely generated by
H(\gamma)(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
we may use
H(\gamma)
to write

H(L)=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,C

where
C=H(L)/I
and
I
is the ideal generated by
H(\gamma)(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T})
. Thus

H(L)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT}\,\widehat{\amalg}\,C,\hskip 28.45274pt\mbox{ and }H(\partial_{\gamma})sx=x.

It follows that

H(H(L)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},H(\partial_{\gamma}))=C

and so
H(L)\to H(H(L)\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT},H(\partial_{\gamma}))
is surjective. This shows that
H(\gamma)
is inert. Moreover, if
H(\gamma)
is inert, this spectral sequence trivially collapses and so

H(j):H(L)\to H(L\,\widehat{\amalg}\,\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{sT})

is also surjective, and
\gamma
is inert.

\square

Corollary.
Suppose
\gamma:(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial)
and
\rho:(\land W,d)\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)
are equivalent attaching maps. Then

(i)

H(\gamma):(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(H(L),0)
and
\rho:(\land W,d_{1})\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)
are also equivalent.

(ii)

If any one of the four attching maps is inert then all are.

proof.
(i) If
(\land W_{L},d_{0}+d_{1})
is the semi-quadratic Sullivan algebra corresponding to
(L,\partial)
, then §17.3 provides the commutative diagram

\textstyle{(\land W_{L},d_{0}+d_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{(\land V_{S},d_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}\oplus S.}

On the other hand (Proposition
12.4
(i)),
H(\gamma):(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(H(L),0)
corresponds to the morphism

(\land(H(W_{L},d_{0}),H(d_{1}))\to(\land V_{S},d_{1}).

This is just
(\land W,d_{1})\to(\land V_{S},d_{1})
, which is equivalent to

\rho(\land W,d_{1})\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S.

(ii) For convenience denote
\rho:(\land W,d_{1})\to(\mathbb{Q}\oplus S,0)
simply by
\rho(1)
. Then combining Proposition
19.1
with Theorem
6
gives

\rho(i)\mbox{ inert}\Longleftrightarrow H(\gamma)\mbox{ inert }\Longleftrightarrow\gamma\mbox{ inert }\Longleftrightarrow\rho\mbox{ inert}.

\square

Remark.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a continuous map from a wedge of spheres
X
. By Theorem 6, when
f
is inert the homotopy of the cofibre
Z
depends only on the map
\pi_{*}(X)\to\pi_{*}(Y)
. This is not the case in general.

As an example denote by
f:S^{2}\to\mathbb{C}P^{3}
the injection of the
2
-skeleton in
\mathbb{C}P^{3}
. The homotopy cofiber is
Z=\mathbb{S}^{4}\vee S^{6}
with homotopy Lie algebra
L_{Z}=\mathbb{L}(x_{3},x_{5})
. Now let
g:S^{2}\to S^{3}\times\mathbb{C}P^{\infty}
denote also the injection of the
2
-skeleton. The maps
\pi_{*}(f)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
and
\pi_{*}(g)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
coincide. The homotopy cofiber of
g
is a formal space
Z^{\prime}
whose cohomology is

\land(x,y,z,t)/(x^{3}-y^{2},zt,tx-yz,ty-x^{2}b),deg\,x=4,deg\,y=6,deg\,z=7,deg\,t=9.

The homotopy Lie algebras
L_{Z}
and
L_{Z^{\prime}}
are clearly different.

21
Characterization of inert Sullivan attaching maps

In this section we fix the following notation, recalling (
17.3
) that
\overline{\rho}^{\vee}:S^{\vee}\to W^{\vee}
is the suspension of
H(\gamma):\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to H(L)
.

\displaystyle\left.\mbox{
\begin{minipage}[398.33858pt]\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]$\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S$ is a surjective cdga morphism from a minimal Sullivan
algebra, where $S=S^{\geq 1}$ is an arbitrary graded vector space and $S\cdot S=0=d(S)$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]$\overline{\rho}:W=\land^{\geq 1}W/\land^{\geq 2}W\to S$ is the surjection induced from $\rho$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]$L_{W}$ is the homotopy Lie algebra of $\land W$ and $T\subset L_{W}$ is the closed subspace defined by $sT=\overline{\rho}^{\vee}(S^{\vee})$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]$(A,d)=\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mbox{ker}\,\overline{\rho}\oplus\land^{\geq 2}W\subset\land W$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]$\varphi:\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}A$ is a minimal Sullivan model.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]$\lambda:\land V\to\land W$ is the composite $\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A\to\land W$, and
$$L_{\lambda}:L_{V}\longleftarrow L_{W}$$is the induced morphism of homotopy Lie algebras.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[$\bullet$]If $\rho$ is inert then the image of $\overline{\rho}^{\vee}:S^{\vee}\to W^{\vee}$ is an \emph{inert subspace} $sT$ of $W^{\vee}=\pi_{*}\langle\land W\rangle$, and $T\subset L_{W}$ is an \emph{inert subspace} of $L_{W}$
\end{enumerate}\end{minipage}
}\right\}

Remark.
The isomorphism (
98
)
is a generalisation of the following result of
[
21
]
: Let
F
be the homotopy fibre of a map
X\to X^{\prime}:=X\cup_{g}D^{n+1}
with
X
simply connected and
n\geq 1
. The boundary
S^{n}=\partial D^{n+1}
then lifts to a map
S^{n}\to F
. Denote by
u\in H_{n}(F;\mathbb{Q})
the image of the fundamental class of
S^{n}
. The holonomy action of
H_{*}(\Omega X^{\prime};\mathbb{Q})
on
H_{*}(F;\mathbb{Q})
then yields an isomorphism
H_{\geq 1}(F;\mathbb{Q})\cong(\mathbb{Q}u)\otimes H_{*}(\Omega X^{\prime};\mathbb{Q})
.

Theorem 7
.

With the hypotheses and notation of (
89
) and (
21
),
the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is inert.

(ii)

\land V\otimes\land Z
is a minimal Sullivan algebra and
\xi
is an isomorphism.

(iii)

For appropriate choice of generating space,
Z
, there is an isomorphism of graded algebras
\mathbb{Q}\oplus(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,\overline{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}H(\land Z,\overline{d}),
i.e. the homotopy Lie algebra,
L_{Z}
, of
\land Z
is profree

proof.

(i
\Longleftrightarrow
(ii). By Theorem
6
,
\rho
is inert if and only if
\lambda
induces an inclusion,

V\to\land^{\geq 1}W/\land^{\geq 2}W.

But this map is an inclusion if and only if
\lambda:\land V\to\land W
is a minimal
\Lambda
-extension, i.e., if and only if
\land V\otimes\land Z
is a minimal Sullivan algebra. And in this case,
\xi
is a quasi-isomorphism of minimal Sullivan algebras, and hence is an isomorphism.

(ii)
\Longrightarrow
(iii) Identify
\land V\otimes\land Z=\land W
via the isomorphism
\xi
. Then denote by
(\land V\otimes\land U,d)
the acyclic closure of
\land V
. As in (
98
), applying
-\otimes_{\land V}\land V\otimes\land U
to the quasi-isomorphism
\xi
yields a quasi-isomorphism

\tau:(\land W\otimes\land U,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(\land Z,\overline{d}).

We show that
\tau
has a right inverse

\sigma:(\land Z,\overline{d})\to(\land W\otimes\land U,d)

in which
\sigma:Z\to\land^{\geq 1}W\otimes\land U
:

In fact, write
Z=\cup_{k}Z_{k}
with
Z_{k+1}=Z\cap d^{-1}(\land V\otimes\land Z_{k})
. Then assume by induction that
\sigma
has been constructed in some
Z_{k}
. This implies that for
z\in Z_{k+1}

\sigma(\overline{d}z)\in\sigma(\land^{\geq 2}Z_{k})\subset\land^{\geq 2}W\otimes\land U.

In particular,
\sigma(\overline{d}z)
is a cycle in
A^{\geq 1}\otimes\land U
which is quasi-isomorphic to
\land^{\geq 1}V\otimes\land U
. Since
H^{\geq 1}(\land V\otimes\land U)=0
,
\sigma(\overline{d}z)=d\Phi
for some
\Phi\in\land W\otimes\land U
.

Next write
\Phi=\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}
with
\Phi_{1}\in\land^{\geq 1}W\otimes\land U
and
\Phi_{2}\in\land U
. Since
d:U\to\land^{\geq 1}V\otimes\land U
, and
\land W
is minimal,
d\Phi_{1}\in\land^{\geq 2}W\otimes\land U
, while also
d\Phi=\sigma(\overline{d}z)\in\land^{\geq 2}W\otimes\land U
. Thus
d\Phi_{2}\in\land^{\geq 2}W\otimes\land U
. But as with any acyclic closure, if
\Phi_{2}\neq 0
then
d\Phi_{2}
has a non-zero component in
V\otimes\land U
. Thus
\Phi_{2}=0
and
\sigma(\overline{d}z)=d\Phi_{1}
. Extend
\sigma
to
Z_{k+1}
by setting
\sigma(z)=\Phi_{1}
for a basis element
z
of a direct summand of
Z_{k}
in
Z_{k+1}
.

Finally, apply
-\otimes_{\land V}\land V\otimes\land U
to the row exact diagram in (
21
). This yields the commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\land Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\land^{\geq 1}Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes\land U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W\otimes\land U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\rho}\otimes id}
\textstyle{S\otimes\land U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

in which
\alpha
is a quasi-isomorphism because
\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A
. It follows that
\overline{\sigma}
is a quasi-isomorphism in which
\overline{\sigma}(\land^{\geq 2}Z)=0
. In particular, the injection
Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,\overline{d}\to\land^{\geq 1}Z
is a quasi-isomorphism, and
\mathbb{Q}\oplus(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,\overline{d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\land Z
.

(iii)
\Longrightarrow
(ii) Here, even if
\land V\otimes\land Z
is not a minimal Sullivan algebra, we have from (
21
) a row-exact commutative diagram of the form

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land V\otimes\land Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\rho}\circ\xi}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\rho}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
\gamma
is surjective,
B\to A
is a quasi-isomorphism. Therefore so is the inclusion
\land V\to B
.

As observed in
[
19
, Proposition 17.9]
, spatial realization provides a fibration,

\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\langle\land V\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\langle\land V\otimes\land Z\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\langle\land Z\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which identifies the sequence
0\leftarrow\pi_{*}\langle\land V\rangle\leftarrow\pi_{*}\langle\land V\otimes\land Z\rangle\leftarrow\pi_{*}\langle\land Z\rangle\leftarrow 0
with the sequence
0\leftarrow V^{\vee}\leftarrow W^{\vee}\leftarrow Z^{\vee}\leftarrow 0
. The second sequence is short exact if and only if
\land V\otimes\land Z
is a minimal Sullivan algebra, while the first is short exact if and only if the connecting homomorphism
\partial:\pi_{*}<\land V\rangle\to\pi_{*-1}\langle\land Z\rangle
is zero.

However, a classical homotopy theory argument shows that if
\partial\alpha\neq 0
then the Whitehead products
Wh(\partial\alpha,\beta)
,
\beta\in\pi_{*}\langle\land Z\rangle
, are all zero. If we identify
\pi_{*}\langle\land Z\rangle=sL_{Z}
then by Proposition
8.2
,
\partial\alpha
is in the center of the profree Lie algebra
L_{Z}
. This implies that
L_{Z}=\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{x}
and dim
\,H^{\geq 1}(\land Z)=1
.

On the other hand, it follows from (
21
) that
H^{\geq 1}(\land Z)\cong S\otimes\land U
.
Thus if dim
\,H^{\geq 1}(\land Z)=1
then
U=0
,
\land V\otimes\land Z=\land Z
and
\land V\otimes\land Z
is minimal.
\square

Corollary 1.
If
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
is inert and the center of
L_{Z}
is non zero then
V=0
,
W=Z
and
L_{Z}
is a free Lie algebra on a single generator.

Corollary 2.

The following conditions on the homotopy Lie algebra,
L_{W}
, of a minimal Sullivan algebra,
\land W
, are equivalent.

(i)

L_{W}
is profree.

(ii)

Each closed direct summand,
T
, of
L_{W}^{(2)}
is inert.

(iii)

Some closed direct summand,
T
, of
L_{W}^{(2)}
is inert.

proof.

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) By Proposition
8.4
we may assume
(\land W,d)
is quadratic and that
W\cap\mbox{ker}\,d\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{\geq 1}(\land W)
. Moreover, by Theorem
1
and the preceding Remarks, the inclusion of
T
in
L_{W}
is dual to a surjection
\rho:W\to W\cap\mbox{ker}\,d
. In particular, for this Sullivan attaching map
H(A)=\mathbb{Q}
,
V=0
, and
T
is inert.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is immediate.

(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i). By Theorem
7
we obtain from the inclusion
T\subset L_{W}
and the corresponding Sullivan attaching map
\rho:\land W\to W\cap\mbox{ker}\,d
, the decomposition

\textstyle{\land V\otimes\land Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{\land W.}

By Lemma
4.1
,
L_{W}
is the closure of the sub Lie algebra generated by
T
. Since
T\subset L_{Z}
it follows that
L_{W}=L_{Z}
,
V=0
and
L_{W}
is profree.

\square

Example.
Suppose

A\to A\oplus\mathbb{Q}y,\hskip 14.22636ptA^{\geq 1}\cdot y=0

is a cdga morphism defined as follows:

\bullet

A
has a quadratic Sullivan model
\varphi:\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A
.

\bullet

dy=\varphi x
where
x\in(\land V)^{m}
and
[x]\in H(\land V)
is non zero.

\bullet

A^{0}=\mathbb{Q}
,
H^{m}(\land V)=\mathbb{Q}x
and
H^{>m}(A)=0
.

Proposition 21.1
.

With the hypotheses above let
\varphi_{W}:\land W\to A\oplus\mathbb{Q}y
be a minimal Sullivan model. Then the composite

\rho:\land W\to A\oplus\mathbb{Q}y\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathbb{Q}y

is inert.

proof.
Since
A\to A\oplus\mathbb{Q}y
is an isomorphism in homology in degrees
<m
and is surjective in homology in degree
m
, the induced Sullivan representative
\land V\to\land W
satisfies
V^{<m}\to W^{<m}
is injective.

Now for any minimal Sullivan algebra
(\land Z,d)
denote by
\rho_{Z}
the restriction
Z^{\vee}\to(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d)^{\vee}
. When the differential
d
is quadratic, then (Lemma
4.1
), the sequence

0\to L_{Z}^{(2)}\to L_{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho_{Z}}}{{\longrightarrow}}s(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d)^{\vee}\to 0

is exact. By hypothesis, in the situation here
V^{k}\cap\mbox{ker}\,d=0
,
k\geq m
. It follows that
L_{V}
is generated as an enriched Lie algebra by elements in degrees
<m-1
.

Since
L_{W}\to L_{V}
is surjective in degrees
<m
, this map is surjective.

\square

Corollary.
Let
X=Y\cup_{S^{m-1}}D^{m}
be a connected CW complex whose minimal Sullivan model
\land V
satisfies
V=V^{1}
. If
m\geq 2
and dim
\,Y<m
, the attaching map
S^{m-1}\to Y
is rationally inert.

22
A third characterization of inert attaching maps

Suppose (§19) that

(\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T},0)\to(L,\partial_{L})\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{ and }(\land W,d)\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S

are equivalent attaching maps. Then (Corollary to Theorem
6
),

\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to H(L)\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{ and }(\land W,d_{1})\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S

are also equivalent, and if any of these attaching maps are inert then all four are inert. Therefore in characterizing inert attaching maps it is sufficient to consider two equivalent attaching maps of the form

\gamma:\overline{\mathbb{L}}_{T}\to(L,0)\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{ and }\rho:(\land W,d_{1})\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S.

Henceforth in this section we fix such an equivalent pair
(\gamma,\rho)
.

Then consistent with the notation in §19, §20, and §21,

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}\bullet&\mbox{ $L=L_{W}$ is the homotopy Lie algebra of $\land W$,}\\
\bullet&\mbox{ $s\gamma=\rho^{\vee}$, and }\\
\bullet&\mbox{ $I\subset L$ is the closed ideal generated by $\gamma(T)$, so that $sI\subset W^{\vee}$.}\end{array}\right\}

Now recall (
21
) that a quasi-isomorphism
\varphi:\land V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A=\mbox{ker}\,\widehat{\rho}
extends to a minimal
\Lambda
-extension

\lambda:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.4722pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.4722pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\land V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.01973pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.4722pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.4722pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 65.4722pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\land V\otimes\land Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 131.11449pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\xi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 129.80893pt\raise-4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 168.8471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 138.8471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 168.8471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\land W}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

in which
\xi|_{\land V}
is the composite
\textstyle{\land V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W}
. Moreover (§21)
\land V\otimes\land Z
is a Sullivan algebra,
\xi
is a surjective quasi-isomorphism and (Theorem
7
)
\xi
is an isomorphism if and only if
\rho
is inert.

Proposition 22.1
.

If
(\gamma,\rho)
is an inert pair, then
\varphi
can be chosen so that
\land V
is quadratic and
\lambda:V\to W.

proof.
Since
\rho:\land W\to\mathbb{Q}\oplus S
preserves wedge degrees it follows that
A=\oplus_{k}A\cap\land^{k}W
. Therefore the morphism
\varphi
can be chosen so that
V=\oplus_{k}V(k)
with
\varphi:V(k)\to A\cap\land^{k}W
. Now if
\rho
is inert then
\lambda(V)\cap\land^{\geq 2}W=0
. Thus in this case
\lambda:V\to W
and
(\land V,d)
is quadratic.
\square

Theorem 8
.

With the hypotheses and notation above and in (
89
), the following conditions on an equivalent pair
(\gamma,\rho)
are equivalent:

(i)

(\gamma,\rho)
is an inert pair.

(ii)

The closed ideal
I
in
L
generated by
\gamma(T)
is profree, and the right adjoint representation of
L/I
in
I/I^{(2)}
induces an isomorphism of
\overline{UL/I}
-modules

T\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\overline{UL/I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}I/I^{(2)},

extending
T\to I\to I/I^{(2)}
.

Corollary.
If
\gamma:\mathbb{Q}x\to L
is an inert attaching map, and if
y\in L
is not a scalar multiple of
x
or of
[x,x]
, then
[x,y]\neq 0
.

proof.
Let
I
be the closed ideal generated by
x
. Then (Proposition
6.2
)
I
is profree. If
y\in I
and if
y\not\in\mathbb{Q}x+\mathbb{Q}[x,x]
then the sub Lie algebra generated by
x
and
y
is free, and
[x,y]\neq 0
.

On the other hand when
y\not\in I
, let
\overline{y}=[y]\in L/I
. Since
I/I^{(2)}
is isomorphic to
x\otimes\overline{U(L_{W}/I)}
via the adjoint representation,
x\cdot\overline{y}=\overline{[{x},y]}
is non zero.

\square

The proof of Theorem 8 occupies the next two subsections. In §22.1 we establish a Proposition which is core of the proof. Then §22.2 applies that Proposition to complete the proof of the Theorem.

22.1
A comparison of holonomy representations for inert pairs
(\gamma,\rho)

In this sub section we fix an arbitrary inert equivalent pair
(\gamma,\rho)
.
Thus
\xi:\land V\otimes\land Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\land W
and

0\leftarrow L_{V}\leftarrow L_{W}\leftarrow L_{Z}\leftarrow 0

is a short exact sequence.

Now the relation
<\lambda V,s\gamma(T)>=<\rho\lambda V,sT>=0
implies that
\gamma:T\to L_{Z}
. Since
L_{Z}
is a closed ideal this implies that

I\subset L_{Z}.

On the other hand, since
\rho(\land^{\geq 1}V)\subset\rho(A^{\geq 1})=0
,
\rho
factors over division by
\land^{\geq 1}V
to define
\rho_{Z}:\land Z\to S\oplus\mathbb{Q}
. Thus restriction of
\rho_{Z}
to
Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}
and composition of
\gamma
with the surjection
L_{Z}\to L_{Z}/L_{Z}^{(2)}
yield linear maps

\overline{\rho_{Z}}:Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}\to S\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and }\overline{\gamma}:T\to L_{Z}/L_{Z}^{(2)}.

Note that by construction,
\overline{\gamma}
is coherent. Now denote by
T\widehat{\otimes}\overline{UL_{V}}
the completion of the enriched vector space
T\otimes\overline{UL_{V}}
(§2).

Proposition 22.2
.

Since
(\gamma,\rho)
is an inert equivalent pair then there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{L_{Z}/L_{Z}^{(2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{T\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\overline{UL_{V}}}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{id\otimes 1}

in which
\chi
is an isomorphism of
\overline{UL_{V}}
-modules, and

\bullet

The representation in
L_{Z}/L_{Z}^{(2)}
is induced by the right adjoint representation of
\overline{UL_{V}}
in
L_{Z}
.

\bullet

The representation in
T\widehat{\otimes}\overline{UL_{V}}
is multiplication from the right.

proof.
The proof is in several Steps. Throughout,
\varepsilon_{V}
and
\varepsilon_{U}
denote the augmentations in
\land V
and
\land U
, with
\varepsilon_{U}(U)=0
.

Step One: The quasi-isomorphism

\varepsilon_{V}\otimes\rho_{Z}:(\land V\otimes(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}),d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(S,0).

Since
\land W
is a quadratic Sullivan algebra it follows that

d:1\otimes Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}\to\land^{2}V\oplus(V\otimes Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}).

Thus division by
\land V\otimes 1
defines (§5.3) a quadratic
\land V
-module
(\land V\otimes(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}),d)
. In particular,

\varepsilon_{V}\otimes\rho_{Z}:\land V\otimes Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}\to S

is a morphism of chain complexes.

On the other hand, the row-exact commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\land V,d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{(\land V\otimes\land Z,d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(\land V\otimes\land^{\geq 1}Z,\overline{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\land W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

shows that
\rho:\land V\otimes\land^{\geq 1}Z\to S
is a quasi-isomorphism. But since
\rho
is inert,
L_{Z}
is profree (Theorem
7
). Therefore (Theorem
1
)
Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z}\to(\land^{\geq 1}Z,d_{Z})
is a quasi-isomorphism. Because
\land W
is quadratic, it follows that
\land V\otimes(Z\cap\mbox{ker}\,d_{Z})
is preserved by
d
, and

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.1343pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-39.1343pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\land V\otimes 54.91437pt\raise 4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 93.1343pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.1343pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 93.1343pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\land V\otimes\land^{\geq 1}Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 160.60303pt\raise 4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern
